Every other exception is\n211\t        captured and folded into an ``EvalOutcome`` with status\n212\t        ``ERRORED``.\n213\t    \"\"\"\n214\t\n215\t    # Per-step exception capture lives in ``_LifecycleState`` so the\n216\t    # status-mapping helper can read every step's result without\n217\t    # threading positional state through the function body.\n218\t    state = _LifecycleState(spec=spec)\n219\t\n220\t    try:\n221\t        # Step 1 — build isolation. ``HomeIsolation.setup`` is itself\n222\t        # idempotency-guarded; the atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) preserves\n223\t        # the partial HOME on failure so ``teardown(keep=True)`` keeps\n224\t        # forensic artifacts on disk.\n225\t        try:\n226\t            home.setup(config=config)\n227\t        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n228\t            raise\n229\t        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n230\t            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"setup\", exc=exc)\n231\t            return _finalize(\n232\t                state=state,\n233\t                home=home,\n234\t                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n235\t                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n236\t            )\n237\t\n238\t        home_path = home.home_path\n239\t\n240\t        # Step 2 — deploy hooks. Build the executor's pre-observe view. ``env`` is taken from\n256\t        # ``HomeIsolation.env()`` so the executor sees the canonical\n257\t        # HOME / CLAUDE_SESSION_ID overlay.\n258\t        exec_ctx = ExecutorContext(\n259\t            eval_spec=spec,\n260\t            home=home,\n261\t            home_path=home_path,\n262\t            run_dir=run_dir,\n263\t            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n264\t            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n265\t            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n266\t            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n267\t            env=MappingProxyType(dict(home.env())),\n268\t        )\n269\t\n270\t        # Step 3 — spawn.\n271\t        try:\n272\t            executor.spawn(exec_ctx)\n273\t        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n274\t            raise\n275\t        except BaseException as exc:  # Step 4 — inject.\n285\t        try:\n286\t            executor.inject(exec_ctx)\n287\t        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n288\t            raise\n289\t        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n290\t            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"inject\", exc=exc)\n291\t            return _finalize(\n292\t                state=state,\n293\t                home=home,\n294\t                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n295\t                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n296\t            )\n297\t\n298\t        # Step 5 — observe.\n299\t        try:\n300\t            observed = executor.observe(exec_ctx)\n301\t        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n302\t            raise\n303\t        except BaseException as exc:  # Step 6 — assert. Build the per-Expect EvalContext now that we\n314\t        # have stdout/stderr/exit_code/duration, then apply each\n315\t        # callable in declaration order. A callable that raises folds\n316\t        # into ERRORED with a partial ``expects`` tuple (whatever\n317\t        # callables ran before the raise).\n318\t        ctx = EvalContext.from_runner_state(\n319\t            eval_spec=spec,\n320\t            home=home,\n321\t            run_dir=run_dir,\n322\t            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n323\t            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n324\t            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n325\t            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n326\t            jsonl_paths=observed.jsonl_paths,\n327\t            env=exec_ctx.env,\n328\t            exit_code=observed.exit_code,\n329\t            stdout=observed.stdout,\n330\t            stderr=observed.stderr,\n331\t            duration_sec=observed.duration_sec,\n332\t            artifacts=observed.artifacts,\n333\t        )\n334\t\n335\t        for callable_ in expect_callables:\n336\t            try:\n337\t                result = callable_(ctx)\n338\t            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n339\t                raise\n340\t            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n341\t                state.record_harness_failure(step=\"assert\", exc=exc)\n342\t                return _finalize(\n343\t                    state=state,\n344\t                    home=home,\n345\t                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n346\t                    keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n347\t                )\n348\t            state.record_expect(result)\n349\t\n350\t        return _finalize(\n351\t            state=state,\n352\t            home=home,\n353\t            on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n354\t            keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n355\t        )\n356\t\n357\t    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n358\t        # NFR-REL1 contract: cooperative cancellation. ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n367\t# Internal state + classification helpers\n368\t# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n369\t\n370\t\n371\t@dataclass\n372\tclass _LifecycleState:\n373\t    \"\"\"Mutable per-call state captured by ``run_eval``.\n374\t\n375\t    Kept private to the module — the public surface is ``EvalOutcome``.\n376\t    The dataclass groups everything the classification helper needs so\n377\t    the body of ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n378\t    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n379\t    \"\"\"\n380\t\n381\t    spec: EvalSpec\n382\t    observed: step\n395\t        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n396\t\n397\t\n398\tdef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n399\t    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n400\t\n401\t    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n402\t\n403\t    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n404\t      fully-qualified classname.\n405\t    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n406\t    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n407\t\n408\t    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n409\t    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n410\t    \"\"\"\n411\t\n412\t    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n413\t        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n414\t        cls = type(exc)\n415\t        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n416\t\n417\t    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n418\t        return \"PASS\", None\n419\t\n420\t    return \"FAIL\", None\n421\t\n422\t\n423\tdef _finalize(\n424\t    *,\n425\t    state: _LifecycleState,\n426\t    home: HomeIsolation,\n427\t    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n428\t    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n429\t) -> EvalOutcome:\n430\t    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n431\t\n432\t    The ``keep`` flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n433\t    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n434\t    status forces ``keep=True`` so failed/errored evals preserve their\n435\t    HOME for forensic inspection. Teardown failures are routed through\n436\t    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n437\t    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n438\t    \"\"\"\n439\t\n440\t    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n441\t\n442\t    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n443\t    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n444\t\n445\t    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. A lock keeps the ordered\n535\t    list of buffered events consistent and `write()` deterministic.\n536\t    \"\"\"\n537\t\n538\t    def __init__(self, *, eval_id: str, clock: Callable[[], float]) -> None:\n539\t        self._eval_id = eval_id\n540\t        self._clock = clock\n541\t        self._start = clock()\n542\t        self._lock = threading.Lock()\n543\t        self._events: list[_LogEvent] = []\n544\t\n545\t    def _ts_offset(self) -> float:\n546\t        return self._clock() - A single public entrypoint\n``run_eval(spec, ...) -> EvalOutcome`` executes the seven-step sequence:\n\n    1. build isolation  (HomeIsolation.setup)\n    2. deploy hooks     (deploy_hooks_to)\n    3. spawn            (executor.spawn)\n    4. inject           (executor.inject)\n    5. observe          (executor.observe)\n    6. assert           (each ExpectCallable applied to an EvalContext)\n    7. teardown         (HomeIsolation.teardown)\n\nand maps the outcome to a DM-001 ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01) following the\nstatus-mapping rules documented in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n* All applied ``ExpectCallable``s returned ``passed=True`` -> ``PASS``.\n* At least one ``ExpectResult.passed`` is ``False`` and no harness\n  exception fired -> ``FAIL``.\n* Any exception other than ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` raised\n  during steps 1-6 -> ``ERRORED`` with ``error_class`` = fully-qualified\n  exception classname.\n* ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` propagates so NFR-REL1\n  (T03.07) can mark the outcome ``INTERRUPTED`` at the orchestrator\n  layer. Teardown still runs with ``keep=True`` so the partial HOME\n  survives for forensics.\n\nThe runner is intentionally testable without a real PTY: the spawn /\ninject / observe trio is supplied as a :class:`LifecycleExecutor` protocol\nso unit tests substitute stubs that return canned outputs. The COMP-004\nrunner class layer (T03.05 / D-0049) plugs in the concrete\n``PtyDriver`` + ``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` executor and adds per-eval JSONL\nlogging and per-eval timeout enforcement.\n\nPer-eval JSONL logging, per-eval timeout, signal handling, and the\nSKIPPED capability-gate branch are explicitly out of scope; see\n``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` for the full out-of-scope list.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport threading\nimport time\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom types import MappingProxyType\nfrom typing import Any, Callable, ClassVar, Mapping, Optional, Protocol, Sequence\n\nfrom .config import EvalConfig\nfrom .hook_adapter import deploy_hooks_to\nfrom .isolation import HomeIsolation\nfrom .models import (\n    EvalContext,\n    EvalOutcome,\n    EvalSpec,\n    EvalStatus,\n    ExpectResult,\n)\nfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"EvalRunner\",\n    \"ExecutorContext\",\n    \"ExpectCallable\",\n    \"LifecycleExecutor\",\n    \"ObservedRun\",\n    \"run_eval\",\n]\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Public protocol surface\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n# An ExpectCallable is a function from ``EvalContext`` to ``ExpectResult``.\n# FR-EXP1 (T04.01..T04.07) lands the concrete primitives; the runner only\n# requires the callable contract today.\nExpectCallable = Callable[[EvalContext], ExpectResult]\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExecutorContext:\n    \"\"\"Post-isolation runtime state the executor needs.\n\n    Carries the paths the runner has reserved for the eval's outputs plus\n    the resolved environment overlay so the executor can spawn the\n    subprocess against the per-eval HOME without re-deriving anything.\n\n    Fields are intentionally minimal — the richer per-Expect\n    :class:`EvalContext` (DM-010 / T03.03) is built *after* observe\n    returns so it can carry the captured stdout/stderr/exit_code.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_spec: EvalSpec\n    home: HomeIsolation\n    home_path: Path\n    run_dir: Path\n    artifacts_dir: Path\n    stdout_path: Path\n    stderr_path: Path\n    transcript_path: Path\n    env: Mapping[str, str]\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ObservedRun:\n    \"\"\"Result of the executor's spawn -> inject -> observe trio.\n\n    The runner takes this and folds it into the :class:`EvalContext` that\n    every :data:`ExpectCallable` receives in step 6.\n\n    * ``exit_code``    — captured subprocess exit code; ``int``.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured (PTY-stripped) stdout.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds from spawn to exit.\n    * ``jsonl_paths``  — mapping of named JSONL log paths produced by the\n      run (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Stored as an\n      immutable :class:`Mapping`.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      :class:`Mapping`.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    exit_code: int\n    stdout: str\n    stderr: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n\nclass LifecycleExecutor(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Strategy that performs the spawn / inject / observe steps.\n\n    Production code (T03.05) implements this on top of\n    ``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` + ``PtyDriver``. Tests substitute a stub\n    that records the call sequence and returns a canned\n    :class:`ObservedRun`. Implementations should raise on failure;\n    ``run_eval`` classifies any non-``KeyboardInterrupt`` /\n    ``SystemExit`` exception as ``ERRORED``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def spawn(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n    def inject(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n    def observe(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> ObservedRun:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Internal sentinel for \"no observed run yet\"\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n_NO_OBSERVED_RUN = ObservedRun(\n    exit_code=-1,\n    stdout=\"\",\n    stderr=\"\",\n    duration_sec=0.0,\n    jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType({}),\n    artifacts=MappingProxyType({}),\n)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Public entrypoint\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef run_eval(\n    spec: EvalSpec,\n    *,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n    run_dir: Path,\n    artifacts_dir: Path,\n    stdout_path: Path,\n    stderr_path: Path,\n    transcript_path: Path,\n    executor: LifecycleExecutor,\n    expect_callables: Sequence[ExpectCallable] = (),\n    deploy_hooks: Callable[[Path], None] = deploy_hooks_to,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]] = None,\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool = False,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Execute the FR-LC1 7-step lifecycle and return an ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The function is the *skeleton* on top of which COMP-004 EvalRunner\n    (T03.05) layers per-eval JSONL logging and per-eval timeout\n    enforcement. See ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` for the full lifecycle\n    + status-mapping contract.\n\n    Arguments are keyword-only (after ``spec``) so future field\n    additions cannot silently re-bind positional callers. The default\n    ``deploy_hooks`` resolves to the COMP-014 ``deploy_hooks_to``\n    function; tests inject a stub.\n\n    Raises\n    ------\n    KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit\n        Re-raised verbatim after running ``home.teardown(keep=True)`` so\n        NFR-REL1 (T03.07) can convert them into an ``INTERRUPTED``\n        outcome at the orchestrator layer. Every other exception is\n        captured and folded into an ``EvalOutcome`` with status\n        ``ERRORED``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Per-step exception capture lives in ``_LifecycleState`` so the\n    # status-mapping helper can read every step's result without\n    # threading positional state through the function body.\n    state = _LifecycleState(spec=spec)\n\n    try:\n        # Step 1 — build isolation. ``HomeIsolation.setup`` is itself\n        # idempotency-guarded; the atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) preserves\n        # the partial HOME on failure so ``teardown(keep=True)`` keeps\n        # forensic artifacts on disk.\n        try:\n            home.setup(config=config)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"setup\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n\n        home_path = home.home_path\n\n        # Step 2 — deploy hooks. Injectable so tests stub the deploy\n        # without a real ``hooks.json`` on disk.\n        try:\n            deploy_hooks(home_path)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"deploy_hooks\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n\n        # Build the executor's pre-observe view. ``env`` is taken from\n        # ``HomeIsolation.env()`` so the executor sees the canonical\n        # HOME / CLAUDE_SESSION_ID overlay.\n        exec_ctx = ExecutorContext(\n            eval_spec=spec,\n            home=home,\n            home_path=home_path,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            env=MappingProxyType(dict(home.env())),\n        )\n\n        # Step 3 — spawn.\n        try:\n            executor.spawn(exec_ctx)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"spawn\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n\n        # Step 4 — inject.\n        try:\n            executor.inject(exec_ctx)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"inject\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n\n        # Step 5 — observe.\n        try:\n            observed = executor.observe(exec_ctx)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"observe\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n        state.record_observed(observed)\n\n        # Step 6 — assert. Build the per-Expect EvalContext now that we\n        # have stdout/stderr/exit_code/duration, then apply each\n        # callable in declaration order. A callable that raises folds\n        # into ERRORED with a partial ``expects`` tuple (whatever\n        # callables ran before the raise).\n        ctx = EvalContext.from_runner_state(\n            eval_spec=spec,\n            home=home,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            jsonl_paths=observed.jsonl_paths,\n            env=exec_ctx.env,\n            exit_code=observed.exit_code,\n            stdout=observed.stdout,\n            stderr=observed.stderr,\n            duration_sec=observed.duration_sec,\n            artifacts=observed.artifacts,\n        )\n\n        for callable_ in expect_callables:\n            try:\n                result = callable_(ctx)\n            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n                raise\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n                state.record_harness_failure(step=\"assert\", exc=exc)\n                return _finalize(\n                    state=state,\n                    home=home,\n                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                    keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n                )\n            state.record_expect(result)\n\n        return _finalize(\n            state=state,\n            home=home,\n            on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n            keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n        )\n\n    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n        # NFR-REL1 contract: cooperative cancellation. Teardown the\n        # partial HOME with ``keep=True`` so forensics survive, then\n        # re-raise so the orchestrator's signal handler can convert this\n        # into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the suite layer.\n        _safe_teardown(home, keep=True, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n        raise\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Internal state + classification helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass _LifecycleState:\n    \"\"\"Mutable per-call state captured by ``run_eval``.\n\n    Kept private to the module — the public surface is ``EvalOutcome``.\n    The dataclass groups everything the classification helper needs so\n    the body of ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    spec: EvalSpec\n    observed: ObservedRun = _NO_OBSERVED_RUN\n    expects: list[ExpectResult] = field(default_factory=list)\n    harness_failure_step: Optional[str] = None\n    harness_failure_exc: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def record_observed(self, observed: ObservedRun) -> None:\n        self.observed = observed\n\n    def record_expect(self, result: ExpectResult) -> None:\n        self.expects.append(result)\n\n    def record_harness_failure(self, *, step: str, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n        self.harness_failure_step = step\n        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n\n\ndef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n\n    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n      fully-qualified classname.\n    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n\n    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n\n    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n        return \"PASS\", None\n\n    return \"FAIL\", None\n\n\ndef _finalize(\n    *,\n    state: _LifecycleState,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The ``keep`` flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n    status forces ``keep=True`` so failed/errored evals preserve their\n    HOME for forensic inspection. Teardown failures are routed through\n    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n\n    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n\n    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. The observed\n    # value (which may be 0.0 for a stub eval) is preserved verbatim\n    # because the Reporter renders it without further interpretation.\n    duration_sec = state.observed.duration_sec\n    if state.harness_failure_step in {\"setup\", \"deploy_hooks\", \"spawn\", \"inject\", \"observe\"}:\n        # Steps 1-5 failures: observe never ran cleanly, so duration is\n        # not meaningful — pin to 0.0 so the Reporter's per-eval row\n        # does not display a stale or misleading value.\n        duration_sec = 0.0\n\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=state.spec.id,\n        title=state.spec.title,\n        status=status,\n        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n        expects=tuple(state.expects),\n        skip_reason=None,\n        skip_flag_triggered=None,\n        artifacts=dict(state.observed.artifacts),\n        error_class=error_class,\n    )\n\n\ndef _safe_teardown(\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    *,\n    keep: bool,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Invoke ``home.teardown`` swallowing any failure.\n\n    Teardown failures are best-effort by design (see\n    ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` and the ``HomeIsolation.teardown``\n    docstring). The optional callback lets COMP-004 (T03.05) record\n    teardown failures into its per-eval JSONL log without re-raising\n    from the lifecycle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        home.teardown(keep=keep)\n    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n        raise\n    except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — swallowed per spec\n        if on_teardown_error is not None:\n            try:\n                on_teardown_error(exc)\n            except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 — callback failure is non-fatal\n                pass\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# COMP-004 EvalRunner class (T03.05 / D-0049)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass _LogEvent:\n    \"\"\"Single JSONL line buffered by :class:`EvalRunner`.\n\n    The buffer holds events until ``home_path`` is known; once setup\n    completes the buffer is flushed to disk and subsequent events are\n    appended directly. Field shape matches the JSONL contract documented\n    in ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    event: str\n    ts_offset_sec: float\n    eval_id: str\n    step: str\n    extra: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        # Build an ordered dict so JSON diff output stays stable; the\n        # round() pin is documented in D-0049/spec.md (\"Rounded to\n        # microseconds so JSON diff output stays compact and stable.\").\n        return {\n            \"event\": self.event,\n            \"ts_offset_sec\": round(self.ts_offset_sec, 6),\n            \"eval_id\": self.eval_id,\n            \"step\": self.step,\n            \"extra\": dict(self.extra),\n        }\n\n\nclass _JsonlLog:\n    \"\"\"Thread-safe buffer + writer for the per-eval JSONL log.\n\n    The runner emits events from both the worker thread (every\n    lifecycle step except timeout teardown) and the main thread (the\n    ``timeout_fired`` + ``outcome`` events). A lock keeps the ordered\n    list of buffered events consistent and `write()` deterministic.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, *, eval_id: str, clock: Callable[[], float]) -> None:\n        self._eval_id = eval_id\n        self._clock = clock\n        self._start = clock()\n        self._lock = threading.Lock()\n        self._events: list[_LogEvent] = []\n\n    def _ts_offset(self) -> float:\n        return self._clock() - self._start\n\n    def emit(self, event: str, *, step: str, extra: Optional[Mapping[str, Any]] = None) -> None:\n        evt = _LogEvent(\n            event=event,\n            ts_offset_sec=self._ts_offset(),\n            eval_id=self._eval_id,\n            step=step,\n            extra=dict(extra or {}),\n        )\n        with self._lock:\n            self._events.append(evt)\n\n    def snapshot(self) -> tuple[_LogEvent, ...]:\n        with self._lock:\n            return tuple(self._events)\n\n    def write_to(self, path: Path) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Materialise the buffered events as one JSON object per line.\n\n        Failures are best-effort: the JSONL log is a forensic artifact,\n        not a load-bearing part of the outcome contract.\n        \"\"\"\n        with self._lock:\n            events = list(self._events)\n        try:\n            path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n            with path.open(\"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n                for evt in events:\n                    fh.write(json.dumps(evt.to_dict(), sort_keys=False))\n                    fh.write(\"\\n\")\n        except OSError:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort log write\n            return\n\n\nclass _LoggingHomeProxy:\n    \"\"\"Duck-typed proxy around ``HomeIsolation`` emitting setup/teardown events.\n\n    ``HomeIsolation`` is a frozen dataclass so we cannot subclass-and-\n    override its setup/teardown methods. Wrapping the instance keeps the\n    skeleton (``run_eval``) untouched while letting ``EvalRunner`` log\n    the four required events at the exact lifecycle boundary.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, home: Any, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        self._home = home\n        self._log = log\n\n    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_SETUP_STARTED, step=\"setup\")\n        try:\n            home_path = self._home.setup(config=config)\n        except BaseException:\n            raise\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"setup\",\n            extra={\"home_path\": str(home_path)},\n        )\n        return home_path\n\n    def teardown(self, keep: bool) -> None:\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n            step=\"teardown\",\n            extra={\"keep\": keep},\n        )\n        try:\n            self._home.teardown(keep)\n        except BaseException as exc:\n            cls = type(exc)\n            self._log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n            raise\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED, step=\"teardown\")\n\n    def env(self) -> Mapping[str, str]:\n        return self._home.env()\n\n    @property\n    def home_path(self) -> Path:\n        return self._home.home_path\n\n\nclass _LoggingExecutor:\n    \"\"\"LifecycleExecutor proxy emitting spawn/inject/observe events.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, executor: LifecycleExecutor, log: _JsonlLog) -> None:\n        self._executor = executor\n        self._log = log\n\n    def spawn(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> None:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED, step=\"spawn\")\n        self._executor.spawn(ctx)\n\n    def inject(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> None:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_INJECT_STARTED, step=\"inject\")\n        self._executor.inject(ctx)\n\n    def observe(self, ctx: \"ExecutorContext\") -> ObservedRun:\n        self._log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED, step=\"observe\")\n        observed = self._executor.observe(ctx)\n        self._log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"observe\",\n            extra={\n                \"exit_code\": observed.exit_code,\n                \"duration_sec\": observed.duration_sec,\n            },\n        )\n        return observed\n\n\ndef _wrap_expect_with_log(\n    callable_: ExpectCallable, *, index: int, log: _JsonlLog\n) -> ExpectCallable:\n    \"\"\"Return a wrapped ExpectCallable that emits assertion events.\n\n    The wrapper preserves the original signature so ``run_eval`` cannot\n    tell it is being observed. The original callable's ``__name__`` is\n    captured into the ``assertion_started`` / ``assertion_completed``\n    event payloads so post-mortem tooling can correlate JSONL rows with\n    DSL primitives.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name = getattr(callable_, \"__name__\", None)\n    if name == \"<lambda>\":\n        name = None\n\n    def _logging_expect(ctx: EvalContext) -> ExpectResult:\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED,\n            step=\"assert\",\n            extra={\"index\": index, \"name\": name},\n        )\n        result = callable_(ctx)\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED,\n            step=\"assert\",\n            extra={\"index\": index, \"name\": name, \"passed\": bool(result.passed)},\n        )\n        return result\n\n    # Preserve attributes so frameworks that introspect __name__ still work.\n    _logging_expect.__wrapped__ = callable_  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n    if name is not None:\n        _logging_expect.__name__ = name\n    return _logging_expect\n\n\nclass EvalRunner:\n    \"\"\"COMP-004 per-eval runner with JSONL logging + timeout enforcement.\n\n    Wraps the FR-LC1 lifecycle skeleton (``run_eval``) in a class that\n    owns two cross-cutting concerns the skeleton intentionally deferred:\n\n    1. A per-eval JSONL log under\n       ``home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl`` covering every\n       lifecycle transition.\n    2. Per-eval timeout enforcement — when\n       ``EvalSpec.timeout_sec`` is set, the runner returns an\n       ``EvalOutcome`` with status ``TIMEOUT`` if the lifecycle does not\n       complete within budget.\n\n    See ``artifacts/D-0049/spec.md`` for the full contract.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    LOG_DIR_RELPATH: ClassVar[Path] = Path(\".eval-logs\")\n\n    # NFR-REL2 (T03.08 / D-0051): the bounded retry policy. Failed evals\n    # are *not* retried automatically; the user re-runs them with\n    # ``superclaude eval run --eval <id>`` after diagnosing. The\n    # constants below pin the contract so future R3-mit work (T05.23)\n    # has a single place to extend: tags carrying ``MCP_FLAKY_TAG`` will\n    # be eligible for one in-process retry once OQ-10 closes. Until\n    # then, ``retry_count`` is required to be exactly\n    # ``DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT`` and any non-zero value is rejected at\n    # construction time. See ``docs/eval/retry.md`` for the user-facing\n    # subset re-run guidance.\n    DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT: ClassVar[int] = 0\n    MCP_FLAKY_TAG: ClassVar[str] = \"MCP-flaky\"\n\n    EVENT_SETUP_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"setup_started\"\n    EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"setup_completed\"\n    EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED: ClassVar[str] = \"hooks_deployed\"\n    EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"spawn_started\"\n    EVENT_INJECT_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"inject_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"observe_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"observe_completed\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"assertion_started\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"assertion_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_started\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR: ClassVar[str] = \"teardown_error\"\n    EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED: ClassVar[str] = \"timeout_fired\"\n    EVENT_INTERRUPTED_FIRED: ClassVar[str] = \"interrupted_fired\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_STARTED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_started\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_COMPLETED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_completed\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_ERROR: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_error\"\n    EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_SKIPPED: ClassVar[str] = \"executor_cancel_skipped\"\n    EVENT_OUTCOME: ClassVar[str] = \"outcome\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        home: HomeIsolation,\n        config: EvalConfig,\n        executor: LifecycleExecutor,\n        run_dir: Path,\n        artifacts_dir: Path,\n        stdout_path: Path,\n        stderr_path: Path,\n        transcript_path: Path,\n        expect_callables: Sequence[ExpectCallable] = (),\n        deploy_hooks: Callable[[Path], None] = deploy_hooks_to,\n        keep_home_on_pass: bool = False,\n        default_timeout_sec: Optional[float] = None,\n        clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n        retry_count: int = DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT,\n    ) -> None:\n        # NFR-REL2 (T03.08): the harness produces a deterministic\n        # single-pass run. ``retry_count`` is accepted as a constructor\n        # parameter so callers can document intent, but until R3-mit\n        # (T05.23) lands the MCP-flaky retry-once branch the only\n        # accepted value is the default. Reject anything else loudly so\n        # an accidental sweep does not silently re-execute evals and\n        # break the orchestrator's N'-vs-K invariant (FR-RPT1 / T03.11).\n        if retry_count != self.DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalRunner retry_count must be {self.DEFAULT_RETRY_COUNT} \"\n                f\"(NFR-REL2 bounded retry policy); got {retry_count!r}. \"\n                \"Re-run failing evals via 'superclaude eval run --eval <id>' \"\n                \"instead — see docs/eval/retry.md.\"\n            )\n\n        self._home = home\n        self._config = config\n        self._executor = executor\n        self._run_dir = run_dir\n        self._artifacts_dir = artifacts_dir\n        self._stdout_path = stdout_path\n        self._stderr_path = stderr_path\n        self._transcript_path = transcript_path\n        self._expect_callables = tuple(expect_callables)\n        self._deploy_hooks = deploy_hooks\n        self._keep_home_on_pass = keep_home_on_pass\n        self._default_timeout_sec = default_timeout_sec\n        self._clock = clock\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n        self._retry_count = retry_count\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Execute the per-eval lifecycle and return an ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n        Wraps :func:`run_eval` with logging proxies + a per-eval timeout\n        budget. The lifecycle itself is unchanged — every step still\n        runs through the FR-LC1 skeleton — so the status mapping rules\n        from ``D-0048/spec.md`` apply verbatim except for the TIMEOUT\n        branch this class adds.\n\n        When a :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07)\n        and the token is already cancelled at entry, the runner skips\n        the lifecycle entirely and returns an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome.\n        Cancellations that arrive mid-flight are observed cooperatively\n        through the orchestrator (T03.15); the runner itself records\n        ``INTERRUPTED`` if the worker raises ``KeyboardInterrupt``\n        (matching the FR-LC1 skeleton's re-raise behaviour) only when a\n        cancellation_token is wired, otherwise propagation is preserved.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        log = _JsonlLog(eval_id=spec.id, clock=self._clock)\n        timeout_sec = self._resolve_timeout(spec)\n\n        # NFR-REL1: cooperative cancellation. If the signal handler has\n        # already flipped the token before we even start the worker,\n        # return INTERRUPTED immediately so the orchestrator does not\n        # waste a HomeIsolation setup on an eval whose result will be\n        # discarded anyway.\n        if self._cancellation_token is not None and self._cancellation_token.is_cancelled():\n            return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n\n        # Holders for the worker thread. ``outcome_holder`` is populated\n        # on clean return; ``exc_holder`` is populated when run_eval\n        # raised (KeyboardInterrupt / SystemExit, mainly — every other\n        # exception is folded into an ERRORED outcome by run_eval\n        # itself).\n        outcome_holder: dict[str, EvalOutcome] = {}\n        exc_holder: dict[str, BaseException] = {}\n\n        logged_home = _LoggingHomeProxy(self._home, log)\n        logged_executor = _LoggingExecutor(self._executor, log)\n        wrapped_expects = tuple(\n            _wrap_expect_with_log(c, index=i, log=log)\n            for i, c in enumerate(self._expect_callables)\n        )\n\n        # The wrapped ``deploy_hooks`` emits ``hooks_deployed`` once the\n        # underlying call returns; the spec does not require this event\n        # but it is the natural place to record that step 2 completed.\n        original_deploy = self._deploy_hooks\n\n        def logged_deploy(home_path: Path) -> None:\n            original_deploy(home_path)\n            log.emit(EvalRunner.EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED, step=\"deploy_hooks\")\n\n        # ``on_teardown_error`` is invoked by ``_safe_teardown`` when\n        # teardown raises. The proxy already logs the failure via\n        # ``EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR``; the callback is a no-op so the\n        # caller-injected behaviour (preserved by FR-LC1) keeps working\n        # if a future caller wires one through ``EvalRunner``.\n        def on_teardown_error(_exc: BaseException) -> None:\n            return None\n\n        def worker() -> None:\n            try:\n                outcome_holder[\"result\"] = run_eval(\n                    spec,\n                    home=logged_home,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n                    config=self._config,\n                    run_dir=self._run_dir,\n                    artifacts_dir=self._artifacts_dir,\n                    stdout_path=self._stdout_path,\n                    stderr_path=self._stderr_path,\n                    transcript_path=self._transcript_path,\n                    executor=logged_executor,  # type: ignore[arg-type]  # duck-typed proxy\n                    expect_callables=wrapped_expects,\n                    deploy_hooks=logged_deploy,\n                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                    keep_home_on_pass=self._keep_home_on_pass,\n                )\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for re-raise\n                exc_holder[\"exc\"] = exc\n\n        # ``daemon=True`` so a hung worker cannot block process exit.\n        # NFR-REL1 (T03.07) wires the PtyDriver kill + zombie reap that\n        # makes a hung worker rare in production.\n        thread = threading.Thread(\n            target=worker,\n            name=f\"EvalRunner[{spec.id}]\",\n            daemon=True,\n        )\n        thread.start()\n        thread.join(timeout=timeout_sec)\n\n        if thread.is_alive():\n            return self._handle_timeout(spec=spec, log=log, timeout_sec=timeout_sec)\n\n        # Worker returned. Either run_eval produced an outcome or raised\n        # an interrupt; the latter must propagate to the orchestrator so\n        # NFR-REL1 can mark INTERRUPTED. Flush the JSONL log first so\n        # the partial events survive the re-raise.\n        if \"exc\" in exc_holder:\n            exc = exc_holder[\"exc\"]\n            # NFR-REL1 (T03.07): when a cancellation token is wired, the\n            # runner converts a worker-thread KeyboardInterrupt /\n            # SystemExit directly into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome so the\n            # orchestrator's ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed loop never\n            # has to translate an exception into a status. Without a\n            # token, propagation is preserved verbatim so the FR-LC1\n            # contract (and the existing ``test_runner_class.py`` AC4\n            # case) stays unchanged.\n            if (\n                self._cancellation_token is not None\n                and isinstance(exc, (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit))\n            ):\n                self._cancellation_token.cancel()\n                return self._make_interrupted_outcome(spec=spec, log=log)\n            self._flush_log(log)\n            raise exc\n\n        outcome = outcome_holder[\"result\"]\n        # Record the terminal classification so post-mortem tooling can\n        # tell whether the run ended cleanly or was reclassified.\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n            step=\"outcome\",\n            extra={\n                \"status\": outcome.status,\n                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n            },\n        )\n        self._flush_log(log)\n        return outcome\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _resolve_timeout(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> Optional[float]:\n        \"\"\"Return the resolved per-eval timeout, or ``None`` for unbounded.\n\n        ``spec.timeout_sec`` takes precedence; if absent or 0 the\n        runner falls back to ``default_timeout_sec``. ``0`` is treated\n        as \"use the default\" rather than \"no timeout\" because a literal\n        zero would always fire and is never useful in practice.\n        \"\"\"\n        spec_timeout = spec.timeout_sec\n        if spec_timeout is not None and spec_timeout > 0:\n            return float(spec_timeout)\n        if self._default_timeout_sec is not None and self._default_timeout_sec > 0:\n            return float(self._default_timeout_sec)\n        return None\n\n    def _handle_timeout(\n        self,\n        *,\n        spec: EvalSpec,\n        log: _JsonlLog,\n        timeout_sec: Optional[float],\n    ) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Emit timeout events, attempt best-effort teardown, return outcome.\n\n        NFR-REL1 (T03.07) extends the T03.05 timeout path with a\n        subprocess kill + zombie reap step: if the wired executor\n        exposes a ``cancel()`` method (the production\n        ``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` + ``PtyDriver`` does), the runner calls\n        it from the main thread so the PtyDriver child terminates and\n        ``wait()`` reaps the zombie before the orchestrator collects\n        the next outcome. Failure is swallowed because the TIMEOUT\n        outcome must always return cleanly; the JSONL log records the\n        cancel attempt.\n        \"\"\"\n        budget = float(timeout_sec) if timeout_sec is not None else 0.0\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED,\n            step=\"timeout\",\n            extra={\"timeout_sec\": budget},\n        )\n\n        # NFR-REL1: kill the executor's subprocess + reap zombie before\n        # we attempt teardown. Documented on D-0050/spec.md.\n        self._cancel_executor(log=log, reason=\"timeout\")\n\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n            step=\"teardown\",\n            extra={\"keep\": True},\n        )\n        try:\n            # The worker may still hold references to the HomeIsolation\n            # instance; HomeIsolation.teardown is idempotent enough that\n            # a main-thread call is safe even if the worker is mid-step.\n            # Failures are swallowed so the TIMEOUT outcome always\n            # returns cleanly.\n            if getattr(self._home, \"is_set_up\", False):\n                self._home.teardown(keep=True)\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — best-effort\n            cls = type(exc)\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\n                    \"error_class\": f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n                    \"message\": str(exc),\n                },\n            )\n\n        outcome = EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"TIMEOUT\",\n            duration_sec=budget,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=\"builtins.TimeoutError\",\n        )\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_OUTCOME,\n            step=\"outcome\",\n            extra={\n                \"status\": outcome.status,\n                \"duration_sec\": outcome.duration_sec,\n                \"error_class\": outcome.error_class,\n            },\n        )\n        self._flush_log(log)\n        return outcome\n\n    def _cancel_executor(self, *, log: _JsonlLog, reason: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Best-effort PtyDriver kill + zombie reap on the wired executor.\n\n        The production executor (``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` driving a\n        ``PtyDriver``) exposes a ``cancel()`` method that calls\n        ``PtyDriver.terminate(force=True)`` followed by ``close()`` so\n        the child process is signalled and ``wait()`` reaps the\n        resulting zombie. Test stubs that do not need cancellation\n        simply omit the method — ``getattr`` returns ``None`` and the\n        runner skips the step.\n\n        Failures are swallowed so a flaky cancel cannot block a\n        TIMEOUT / INTERRUPTED outcome from returning. The JSONL log\n        records ``executor_cancel_*`` events under the ``timeout``\n        step so post-mortem tooling can confirm the kill attempt\n        landed.\n        \"\"\"\n        cancel = getattr(self._executor, \"cancel\", None)\n        if cancel is None or not callable(cancel):\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_SKIPPED,\n                step=reason,\n                extra={\"reason\": \"executor has no cancel() method\"},\n            )\n            return\n        log.emit(\n            EvalRunner.EVENT_EXECUTOR_CANCEL_STARTED,\n            step=reason,\n            extra={},\n        )\n        try:\n            cancel()\n        except BaseException as exc:  # Best-effort cancel: if the executor was mid-flight when the\n        # interrupt arrived, kill its subprocess + reap zombie.\n        self._cancel_executor(log=log, reason=\"interrupt\")\n        # Best-effort teardown so a partial HOME (forensic artefact) is\n        # preserved when isolation already ran.\n        if getattr(self._home, \"is_set_up\", False):\n            log.emit(\n                EvalRunner.EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED,\n                step=\"teardown\",\n                extra={\"keep\": True},\n            )\n            try:\n                self._home.teardown(keep=True)\n            except BaseException as exc:  # The JSONL log is a forensic artifact, never\n        load-bearing for outcome classification.\n        \"\"\"\n        home_path = self._resolve_home_path()\n        if home_path is None:\n            return\n        # ``eval_id`` is regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 upstream so it is safe\n        # to use verbatim in a filename. We still take the first event\n        # for the id to avoid trusting any later mutation.\n        snapshot = log.snapshot()\n        if not snapshot:\n            return\n        eval_id = snapshot[0].eval_id\n        log_path = home_path / self.LOG_DIR_RELPATH / f\"{eval_id}.jsonl\"\n        log.write_to(log_path)\n\n    def _resolve_home_path(self) -> Optional[Path]:\n        \"\"\"Return the per-eval HOME path if available, ``None`` otherwise.\n\n        ``HomeIsolation.home_path`` raises when setup has not run; we\n        catch that and return ``None`` so a setup-time failure never\n        promotes itself into a runner exception.\n        \"\"\"\n        try:\n            return Path(self._home.home_path)\n        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — home_path may raise pre-setup\n            return None\n","numLines":1187,"startLine":1,"totalLines":1187}}}

Setting the token causes\n  in-flight runners to stop honouring new work; the orchestrator then\n  produces ``INTERRUPTED`` :class:`EvalOutcome` rows for every eval that\n  had started but not finished.\n* :class:`SignalHandlerInstaller` — context manager that binds\n  ``SIGINT``/``SIGTERM`` to a callback that sets the cancellation token\n  (and optionally invokes an on-signal callback for partial-summary\n  bookkeeping). Restores the previous handlers on exit so the harness\n  never leaves the process with a permanently re-bound signal handler.\n* :data:`EXIT_INTERRUPTED` — process exit code 3 used when SIGINT/SIGTERM\n  cancels a run, per design-spec §4 / NFR-REL1.\n\nThe signal handler does *not* itself terminate any subprocess. The\nper-eval timeout path inside :class:`EvalRunner` is responsible for\nkilling the underlying :class:`PtyDriver` subprocess and reaping its\nzombie; the signal handler only flips the cooperative flag so the\norchestrator can stop scheduling new evals and the in-flight ones can be\nclassified as ``INTERRUPTED`` once they exit (whether cleanly or via the\nsubprocess kill).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport signal\nimport threading\nfrom types import FrameType\nfrom typing import Callable, Iterable, Optional\n\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"EXIT_INTERRUPTED\",\n    \"DEFAULT_INTERRUPT_SIGNALS\",\n    \"CancellationToken\",\n    \"SignalHandlerInstaller\",\n]\n\n\n# Per design-spec §4 (Exit codes table) and NFR-REL1: SIGINT / SIGTERM\n# during a run terminates with exit code 3. The constant lives here so\n# the orchestrator (T03.15) and the CLI dispatcher (Phase 4) import a\n# single source of truth.\nEXIT_INTERRUPTED: int = 3\n\n\n# ``SIGINT`` and ``SIGTERM`` are both wired to the same cancellation\n# action per design-spec §12 \"Signal handling\". ``SIGINT`` is the Ctrl-C\n# path; ``SIGTERM`` is the supervisor / container path.\nDEFAULT_INTERRUPT_SIGNALS: tuple[int, ...] = (signal.SIGINT, signal.SIGTERM)\n\n\nclass CancellationToken:\n    \"\"\"Thread-safe cooperative cancellation flag.\n\n    Wraps :class:`threading.Event` so producers can ``cancel()`` the\n    token from the signal handler (or from a unit test) and consumers\n    can poll ``is_cancelled()`` or block on ``wait(timeout)``. The flag\n    is one-shot — once cancelled the token stays cancelled for the rest\n    of its lifetime; the harness allocates a fresh token per run rather\n    than resetting an in-use one. The optional ``signum_holder``\n    attribute records the signal number that triggered cancellation so\n    downstream consumers can include it in the partial summary.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\"_event\", \"_signum\", \"_signum_lock\")\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self._event = threading.Event()\n        self._signum: Optional[int] = None\n        self._signum_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n    def cancel(self, *, signum: Optional[int] = None) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Flip the token to the cancelled state.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        signum:\n            Optional signal number that caused the cancellation. Stored\n            on the token so the orchestrator / reporter can record\n            ``\"interrupted_by\"`` in the partial summary. Only the first\n            ``cancel()`` call captures the signum; subsequent calls\n            return ``False`` without overwriting.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        bool\n            ``True`` if this call was the one that flipped the flag,\n            ``False`` if the token was already cancelled. Lets the\n            orchestrator distinguish \"I cancelled\" from \"I observed\n            an already-cancelled token\" without racing against a\n            second signal.\n        \"\"\"\n        first = not self._event.is_set()\n        if first:\n            with self._signum_lock:\n                if not self._event.is_set():\n                    if signum is not None:\n                        self._signum = int(signum)\n                    self._event.set()\n                else:\n                    first = False\n        return first\n\n    def is_cancelled(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` once the token has been cancelled.\"\"\"\n        return self._event.is_set()\n\n    def wait(self, timeout: Optional[float] = None) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Block until cancelled or ``timeout`` elapses.\n\n        Returns ``True`` if the token is cancelled when the call\n        returns, ``False`` on timeout. Matches the\n        :meth:`threading.Event.wait` contract so callers familiar with\n        ``Event`` semantics need no adapter.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._event.wait(timeout)\n\n    @property\n    def signum(self) -> Optional[int]:\n        \"\"\"The signal number that caused cancellation, if any.\"\"\"\n        with self._signum_lock:\n            return self._signum\n\n\n# Signal-handler callback signature: ``(signum, frame, token)`` — matches\n# the stdlib :class:`signal.signal` callback plus the token so callers\n# can implement extra bookkeeping (partial-summary writing, telemetry)\n# without keeping a global token reference.\nOnSignalCallback = Callable[[int, Optional[FrameType], \"CancellationToken\"], None]\n\n\nclass SignalHandlerInstaller:\n    \"\"\"Context manager binding ``SIGINT``/``SIGTERM`` to a cancellation token.\n\n    On ``__enter__`` the installer registers a handler for each signal\n    in ``signals`` that:\n\n    1. Calls ``token.cancel(signum=...)`` so cooperative consumers wake\n       up immediately.\n    2. Invokes the optional ``on_signal`` callback if provided. Failures\n       inside the callback are swallowed — signal handlers must always\n       return cleanly because the runtime may re-enter Python at a\n       restricted point. Use the callback for *bookkeeping only*\n       (logging, writing a partial-summary stub); the heavy work of\n       writing the final partial summary belongs in the orchestrator\n       once the main thread returns from its blocking join.\n\n    On ``__exit__`` the previous handlers (captured on entry) are\n    restored. Installing twice nests cleanly: the inner installer\n    captures the outer installer's handler and restores it on exit.\n\n    Limitations\n    -----------\n\n    Python's :func:`signal.signal` may only be called from the main\n    thread of the main interpreter. Constructing this installer outside\n    the main thread raises :class:`ValueError`. Tests that exercise the\n    handler path must run on the main thread or substitute a stub.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        token: CancellationToken,\n        *,\n        signals: Iterable[int] = DEFAULT_INTERRUPT_SIGNALS,\n        on_signal: Optional[OnSignalCallback] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        self._token = token\n        self._signals: tuple[int, ...] = tuple(int(s) for s in signals)\n        if not self._signals:\n            raise ValueError(\"at least one signal number is required\")\n        self._on_signal = on_signal\n        self._previous: dict[int, object] = {}\n        self._installed = False\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Context-manager surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"SignalHandlerInstaller\":\n        self.install()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        self.restore()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Explicit install / restore (for callers that cannot use ``with``)\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def install(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Bind the handler for every configured signal.\n\n        Idempotent: calling install twice is a no-op on the second call\n        (the previous-handler snapshot is taken once).\n        \"\"\"\n        if self._installed:\n            return\n        if threading.current_thread() is not threading.main_thread():\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"SignalHandlerInstaller must be installed from the main thread\"\n            )\n        for signum in self._signals:\n            prev = signal.signal(signum, self._make_handler())\n            self._previous[signum] = prev\n        self._installed = True\n\n    def restore(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Restore the previous handler for every configured signal.\n\n        Idempotent: safe to call without a prior ``install()``. If\n        :func:`signal.signal` fails (e.g. the previous handler captured\n        a C-level extension that cannot be re-registered from Python)\n        the per-signal failure is swallowed so the harness shutdown\n        path stays clean.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self._installed:\n            return\n        for signum in self._signals:\n            prev = self._previous.get(signum, signal.SIG_DFL)\n            try:\n                signal.signal(signum, prev)  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n            except (ValueError, OSError):  # pragma: no cover - defensive\n                # Restoration is best-effort; failure here cannot block\n                # shutdown.\n                pass\n        self._previous.clear()\n        self._installed = False\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _make_handler(self) -> Callable[[int, Optional[FrameType]], None]:\n        token = self._token\n        on_signal = self._on_signal\n\n        def _handler(signum: int, frame: Optional[FrameType]) -> None:\n            token.cancel(signum=signum)\n            if on_signal is None:\n                return\n            try:\n                on_signal(signum, frame, token)\n            except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 — signal handlers must not raise\n                # Bookkeeping callbacks are best-effort; swallow any\n                # failure so the runtime never re-raises from inside the\n                # signal handler context.\n                return\n\n        return _handler\n","numLines":256,"startLine":1,"totalLines":256}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PaRcAWvDgUjbczV4CMD9vR","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0050 — NFR-REL1 Signal Handling and Per-Eval Cancellation\n2\t\n3\t| Field | Value |\n4\t|---|---|\n5\t| Task | T03.07 |\n6\t| Component(s) | `signal_handler.py`, `runner.py` (EvalRunner) |\n7\t| Roadmap | R-050 (NFR-REL1) |\n8\t| Spec refs | design-spec §4 (Exit codes), §12 (Signal handling), §13 (Reliability), FR-LC1, NFR-REL1 |\n9\t| Depends on | D-0048 (FR-LC1 skeleton), D-0049 (EvalRunner class), D-0036 (PtyDriver) |\n10\t| Consumed by | D-0058/D-0059 (Orchestrator T03.15), D-0090 (Reporter partial summary), Phase-4 CLI dispatcher |\n11\t\n12\t## 1. Goal\n13\t\n14\tProvide the *cancellation primitives* the harness needs so that:\n15\t\n16\t* Pressing `Ctrl-C` (SIGINT) or receiving SIGTERM during a parallel\n17\t  run cancels every in-flight eval, classifies them as `INTERRUPTED`,\n18\t  lets the orchestrator write a partial summary, and surfaces process\n19\t  exit code `3` to the CLI shell.\n20\t* When an individual eval exceeds its `EvalSpec.timeout_sec` budget the\n21\t  runner kills the underlying PTY subprocess and reaps the zombie so the\n22\t  next eval in the queue starts in a clean process table, producing an\n23\t  `EvalOutcome` with status `TIMEOUT`.\n24\t\n25\tThis deliverable ships the *primitives*. Wiring the primitives into the\n26\tCLI entrypoint (signal handler installation around the orchestrator) and\n27\tthe partial-summary writer belongs to T03.15 / Phase 4.\n28\t\n29\t## 2. Public Surface\n30\t\n31\t```python\n32\tfrom superclaude.cli.eval.signal_handler import (\n33\t    EXIT_INTERRUPTED,            # int = 3\n34\t    DEFAULT_INTERRUPT_SIGNALS,   # (SIGINT, SIGTERM)\n35\t    CancellationToken,\n36\t    SignalHandlerInstaller,\n37\t)\n38\tfrom superclaude.cli.eval.runner import EvalRunner\n39\t```\n40\t\n41\t### 2.1 `CancellationToken`\n42\t\n43\tThread-safe cooperative cancellation flag wrapping\n44\t`threading.Event`. One-shot: once cancelled, the token stays cancelled\n45\tfor the rest of its lifetime; the harness allocates a fresh token per\n46\trun rather than resetting.\n47\t\n48\t| Method | Contract |\n49\t|---|---|\n50\t| `cancel(*, signum=None) -> bool` | Flip to cancelled. Returns `True` on the first call, `False` thereafter. First call captures `signum` (read via `.signum`); subsequent calls do **not** overwrite. |\n51\t| `is_cancelled() -> bool` | Non-blocking poll. |\n52\t| `wait(timeout=None) -> bool` | Blocking wait honouring `Event.wait` semantics. |\n53\t| `.signum -> Optional[int]` | The signal number that triggered cancellation, if any. |\n54\t\n55\t### 2.2 `SignalHandlerInstaller`\n56\t\n57\tContext manager binding configured signals (default\n58\t`SIGINT, SIGTERM`) to a handler that:\n59\t\n60\t1. Calls `token.cancel(signum=signum)`.\n61\t2. Invokes the optional `on_signal(signum, frame, token)` callback.\n62\t   Failures inside the callback are swallowed because signal handlers\n63\t   must always return cleanly.\n64\t\n65\tOn `__exit__` / `restore()` the previous handlers (captured at install\n66\ttime) are restored — installing twice is idempotent.\n67\t\n68\tConstraints:\n69\t\n70\t* Must be installed from the main thread. Off-main-thread install raises\n71\t  `ValueError` (mirrors `signal.signal`'s own restriction).\n72\t* Empty `signals` iterable rejected with `ValueError`.\n73\t* `restore()` is a no-op when called without a prior `install()`.\n74\t\n75\t### 2.3 `EvalRunner` integration\n76\t\n77\t`EvalRunner.__init__` accepts a new optional kwarg\n78\t`cancellation_token: CancellationToken | None`. When wired:\n79\t\n80\t| Trigger | Behaviour | Outcome |\n81\t|---|---|---|\n82\t| Token already cancelled at `run()` entry | Skip the lifecycle entirely; do **not** call `home.setup` or `executor.spawn`; emit `interrupted_fired` + `outcome` events; flush log. | `INTERRUPTED`, `duration_sec=0.0`, `error_class=None` |\n83\t| Worker thread raises `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` | Set the token (defensive) and convert into an INTERRUPTED outcome; invoke best-effort `executor.cancel()` and `home.teardown(keep=True)`; flush log. | `INTERRUPTED` |\n84\t| Per-eval timeout expires (`thread.join(timeout)` returns alive) | Emit `timeout_fired`; invoke `executor.cancel()` from the main thread to kill the PtyDriver + reap zombie; emit `executor_cancel_*` events; run `home.teardown(keep=True)`; flush log. | `TIMEOUT`, `error_class=\"builtins.TimeoutError\"`, `duration_sec=timeout_sec` |\n85\t\n86\tIf `cancellation_token` is **not** wired, the pre-existing FR-LC1 /\n87\tT03.05 contract is preserved verbatim — `KeyboardInterrupt` propagates\n88\tto the caller (regression-tested by\n89\t`test_keyboard_interrupt_in_expect_propagates`).\n90\t\n91\t`executor.cancel()` is duck-typed via `getattr(self._executor, \"cancel\", None)`:\n92\t\n93\t* Production executor (`ClaudeProcessAdapter` driving `PtyDriver`)\n94\t  exposes a `cancel()` that issues `PtyDriver.terminate(force=True)`\n95\t  followed by `close()` so the child receives SIGKILL and `wait()` reaps\n96\t  the resulting zombie.\n97\t* Test stubs / executors without cancellation simply omit the method;\n98\t  the runner records an `executor_cancel_skipped` event in the JSONL log\n99\t  and continues.\n100\t\n101\tAll cancel failures are swallowed: a TIMEOUT or INTERRUPTED outcome must\n102\talways return cleanly so the orchestrator can collect it. The error is\n103\trecorded as `executor_cancel_error` with `error_class` + `message`.\n104\t\n105\t## 3. New JSONL Events (additive)\n106\t\n107\tRecorded by `EvalRunner` in the per-eval JSONL log under\n108\t`home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl`. Goal\n\nProvide the *cancellation primitives* the harness needs so that:\n\n* Pressing `Ctrl-C` (SIGINT) or receiving SIGTERM during a parallel\n  run cancels every in-flight eval, classifies them as `INTERRUPTED`,\n  lets the orchestrator write a partial summary, and surfaces process\n  exit code `3` to the CLI shell.\n* When an individual eval exceeds its `EvalSpec.timeout_sec` budget the\n  runner kills the underlying PTY subprocess and reaps the zombie so the\n  next eval in the queue starts in a clean process table, producing an\n  `EvalOutcome` with status `TIMEOUT`.\n\nThis deliverable ships the *primitives*. Wiring the primitives into the\nCLI entrypoint (signal handler installation around the orchestrator) and\nthe partial-summary writer belongs to T03.15 / Phase 4.\n\n## 2. Public Surface\n\n```python\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.signal_handler import (\n    EXIT_INTERRUPTED,            # int = 3\n    DEFAULT_INTERRUPT_SIGNALS,   # (SIGINT, SIGTERM)\n    CancellationToken,\n    SignalHandlerInstaller,\n)\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.runner import EvalRunner\n```\n\n### 2.1 `CancellationToken`\n\nThread-safe cooperative cancellation flag wrapping\n`threading.Event`. One-shot: once cancelled, the token stays cancelled\nfor the rest of its lifetime; the harness allocates a fresh token per\nrun rather than resetting.\n\n| Method | Contract |\n|---|---|\n| `cancel(*, signum=None) -> bool` | Flip to cancelled. Returns `True` on the first call, `False` thereafter. First call captures `signum` (read via `.signum`); subsequent calls do **not** overwrite. |\n| `is_cancelled() -> bool` | Non-blocking poll. |\n| `wait(timeout=None) -> bool` | Blocking wait honouring `Event.wait` semantics. |\n| `.signum -> Optional[int]` | The signal number that triggered cancellation, if any. |\n\n### 2.2 `SignalHandlerInstaller`\n\nContext manager binding configured signals (default\n`SIGINT, SIGTERM`) to a handler that:\n\n1. Calls `token.cancel(signum=signum)`.\n2. Invokes the optional `on_signal(signum, frame, token)` callback.\n   Failures inside the callback are swallowed because signal handlers\n   must always return cleanly.\n\nOn `__exit__` / `restore()` the previous handlers (captured at install\ntime) are restored — installing twice is idempotent.\n\nConstraints:\n\n* Must be installed from the main thread. Off-main-thread install raises\n  `ValueError` (mirrors `signal.signal`'s own restriction).\n* Empty `signals` iterable rejected with `ValueError`.\n* `restore()` is a no-op when called without a prior `install()`.\n\n### 2.3 `EvalRunner` integration\n\n`EvalRunner.__init__` accepts a new optional kwarg\n`cancellation_token: CancellationToken | None`. When wired:\n\n| Trigger | Behaviour | Outcome |\n|---|---|---|\n| Token already cancelled at `run()` entry | Skip the lifecycle entirely; do **not** call `home.setup` or `executor.spawn`; emit `interrupted_fired` + `outcome` events; flush log. | `INTERRUPTED`, `duration_sec=0.0`, `error_class=None` |\n| Worker thread raises `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` | Set the token (defensive) and convert into an INTERRUPTED outcome; invoke best-effort `executor.cancel()` and `home.teardown(keep=True)`; flush log. | `INTERRUPTED` |\n| Per-eval timeout expires (`thread.join(timeout)` returns alive) | Emit `timeout_fired`; invoke `executor.cancel()` from the main thread to kill the PtyDriver + reap zombie; emit `executor_cancel_*` events; run `home.teardown(keep=True)`; flush log. | `TIMEOUT`, `error_class=\"builtins.TimeoutError\"`, `duration_sec=timeout_sec` |\n\nIf `cancellation_token` is **not** wired, the pre-existing FR-LC1 /\nT03.05 contract is preserved verbatim — `KeyboardInterrupt` propagates\nto the caller (regression-tested by\n`test_keyboard_interrupt_in_expect_propagates`).\n\n`executor.cancel()` is duck-typed via `getattr(self._executor, \"cancel\", None)`:\n\n* Production executor (`ClaudeProcessAdapter` driving `PtyDriver`)\n  exposes a `cancel()` that issues `PtyDriver.terminate(force=True)`\n  followed by `close()` so the child receives SIGKILL and `wait()` reaps\n  the resulting zombie.\n* Test stubs / executors without cancellation simply omit the method;\n  the runner records an `executor_cancel_skipped` event in the JSONL log\n  and continues.\n\nAll cancel failures are swallowed: a TIMEOUT or INTERRUPTED outcome must\nalways return cleanly so the orchestrator can collect it. The error is\nrecorded as `executor_cancel_error` with `error_class` + `message`.\n\n## 3. Exit Code Contract\n\n`EXIT_INTERRUPTED = 3` is exported so the CLI dispatcher (Phase 4) and\nthe orchestrator (T03.15) share a single source of truth. Mapping per\ndesign-spec §4:\n\n| Condition | Exit code |\n|---|---|\n| All evals PASS (or XFAIL / SKIPPED only) | 0 |\n| At least one FAIL / XPASS / TIMEOUT / ERRORED | 1 |\n| Harness setup error (schema, capability gate, scratch root) | 2 |\n| SIGINT / SIGTERM observed during a run | 3 |\n\nThis deliverable owns *only* the `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` constant + the\nCancellationToken/SignalHandlerInstaller wiring. The dispatcher that\ncalls `sys.exit(3)` lands in Phase 4.\n\n## 5. Out of Scope\n\n* **Partial-summary writing.** The reporter\n  (COMP-008 / T03.13) is responsible for persisting the interrupted run\n  summary. `SignalHandlerInstaller.on_signal` is the hook the\n  orchestrator can use to call into the reporter, but no concrete\n  writer ships here.\n* **Orchestrator wiring.** Allocating the shared `CancellationToken`,\n  installing the handler around the ThreadPoolExecutor, and translating\n  cancelled futures into outcomes belongs to T03.15.\n* **Retry policy.** NFR-REL2 (T03.08) builds on top of these\n  primitives.\n* **`signal.alarm`-based per-eval timeout.** The runner already uses a\n  `threading.Thread.join(timeout=...)` strategy (T03.05) that does not\n  hold the GIL or interfere with the main-thread signal handler.\n  Switching to `signal.alarm` would conflict with the signal handler\n  installer; we explicitly do not use it.\n\n## 6. Whether\n   the subprocess actually honors the variable is gated on OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8\n   / T06.03); keeping the offset opt-in via non-zero values means the\n   env dict stays minimal until OQ-8 resolves.\n\nCOMP-006 method surface (T02.07) — FR-ISO1\n==========================================\n\nThe four methods below are the externally observable surface of\nCOMP-006. They mutate process-local filesystem state (and an internal\nprivate slot tracking the created HOME path) without ever mutating the\nfour DM-006 fields:\n\n* :meth:`setup`                 — ``mkdtemp`` under ``home_root`` and stash\n  the created path on a private ``_home_path`` slot via\n  ``object.__setattr__``. Returns the created path. Idempotency is one-\n  shot per instance: calling :meth:`setup` twice raises ``RuntimeError``\n  rather than silently leaking a directory.\n* :meth:`env`                   — returns the env-var dict the subprocess\n  needs: ``HOME`` always, ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` always, and\n  ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when ``time_offset_sec != 0``. The\n  return type pins ``dict[str, str]`` so callers can ``.update()`` it\n  alongside ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` without coercion.\n* :meth:`teardown`              — removes the per-eval HOME when ``keep``\n  is false, leaves it on disk when ``keep`` is true (so post-mortem\n  inspection or NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` tagging in T02.13 can still\n  pick the directory up). Always clears the private path slot so the\n  instance is safe to garbage-collect.\n* :meth:`state_path`            — joins ``suffix`` under the active\n  per-eval HOME so consumers (hook adapter T02.14, atomic setup T02.13,\n  artifact writers) never need to import :mod:`os.path` themselves.
