Each row preserves the\n109\tD-0049 five-field shape (`event`, `ts_offset_sec`, `eval_id`, `step`,\n110\t`extra`).\n111\t\n112\t| Event | Step | Extra fields | Emitted when |\n113\t|---|---|---|---|\n114\t| `timeout_fired` | `\"timeout\"` | `{\"timeout_sec\": float}` | Per-eval timeout budget exceeded. |\n115\t| `interrupted_fired` | `\"interrupt\"` | `{}` | Pre-cancelled token detected or worker `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` observed with token wired. |\n116\t| `executor_cancel_started` | `\"timeout\"` / `\"interrupt\"` | `{}` | Runner is about to call `executor.cancel()`. |\n117\t| `executor_cancel_completed` | `\"timeout\"` / `\"interrupt\"` | `{}` | `cancel()` returned without raising. |\n118\t| `executor_cancel_error` | `\"timeout\"` / `\"interrupt\"` | `{\"error_class\": str, \"message\": str}` | `cancel()` raised; failure is swallowed. |\n119\t| `executor_cancel_skipped` | `\"timeout\"` / `\"interrupt\"` | `{\"reason\": str}` | Executor has no `cancel` method. |\n120\t| `outcome` | `\"outcome\"` | `{\"status\": str, \"duration_sec\": float, \"error_class\": Optional[str]}` | Terminal classification (`INTERRUPTED` / `TIMEOUT` / `PASS` / `FAIL` / `ERRORED`). |\n121\t\n122\tExisting T03.05 events (`setup_*`, `spawn_*`, `inject_*`, `observe_*`,\n123\t`assertion_*`, `teardown_*`) are unchanged.\n124\t\n125\t## 4. Exit Code Contract\n126\t\n127\t`EXIT_INTERRUPTED = 3` is exported so the CLI dispatcher (Phase 4) and\n128\tthe orchestrator (T03.15) share a single source of truth. Mapping per\n129\tdesign-spec §4:\n130\t\n131\t| Condition | Exit code |\n132\t|---|---|\n133\t| All evals PASS (or XFAIL / SKIPPED only) | 0 |\n134\t| At least one FAIL / XPASS / TIMEOUT / ERRORED | 1 |\n135\t| Harness setup error (schema, capability gate, scratch root) | 2 |\n136\t| SIGINT / SIGTERM observed during a run | 3 |\n137\t\n138\tThis deliverable owns *only* the `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` constant + the\n139\tCancellationToken/SignalHandlerInstaller wiring. The dispatcher that\n140\tcalls `sys.exit(3)` lands in Phase 4.\n141\t\n142\t## 5. Out of Scope\n143\t\n144\t* **Partial-summary writing.** The reporter\n145\t  (COMP-008 / T03.13) is responsible for persisting the interrupted run\n146\t  summary. `SignalHandlerInstaller.on_signal` is the hook the\n147\t  orchestrator can use to call into the reporter, but no concrete\n148\t  writer ships here.\n149\t* **Orchestrator wiring.** Allocating the shared `CancellationToken`,\n150\t  installing the handler around the ThreadPoolExecutor, and translating\n151\t  cancelled futures into outcomes belongs to T03.15.\n152\t* **Retry policy.** NFR-REL2 (T03.08) builds on top of these\n153\t  primitives.\n154\t* **`signal.alarm`-based per-eval timeout.** The runner already uses a\n155\t  `threading.Thread.join(timeout=...)` strategy (T03.05) that does not\n156\t  hold the GIL or interfere with the main-thread signal handler.\n157\t  Switching to `signal.alarm` would conflict with the signal handler\n158\t  installer; we explicitly do not use it.\n159\t\n160\t## 6. Failure Modes & Containment\n161\t\n162\t* **Token cancelled mid-step.** The runner does not interrupt the\n163\t  worker thread synchronously. The token's role is to (a) prevent the\n164\t  orchestrator scheduling further evals and (b) let the runner detect\n165\t  the worker's eventual `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` and convert\n166\t  it to `INTERRUPTED`. PtyDriver kill is the lever that actually unblocks\n167\t  a stuck `expect()` call.\n168\t* **Executor `cancel()` raises.** Logged as `executor_cancel_error`;\n169\t  TIMEOUT / INTERRUPTED outcome still returns. The zombie may persist,\n170\t  but the harness exit will reap it via the daemon-thread\n171\t  `Thread.join` semantics.\n172\t* **Handler called from non-main thread.** `SignalHandlerInstaller`\n173\t  refuses to install (Python restriction); the orchestrator must\n174\t  install before spawning worker threads.\n175\t* **Restoration failure.** Best-effort: `signal.signal` errors during\n176\t  `restore()` are swallowed so harness shutdown is never blocked.\n177\t\n178\t## 7. The error is\nrecorded as `executor_cancel_error` with `error_class` + `message`.\n\n## 3. New JSONL Events (additive)\n\nRecorded by `EvalRunner` in the per-eval JSONL log under\n`home_path/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl`. Each row preserves the\nD-0049 five-field shape (`event`, `ts_offset_sec`, `eval_id`, `step`,\n`extra`).\n\n| Event | Step | Extra fields | Emitted when |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `timeout_fired` | `\"timeout\"` | `{\"timeout_sec\": float}` | Per-eval timeout budget exceeded. |\n| `interrupted_fired` | `\"interrupt\"` | `{}` | Pre-cancelled token detected or worker `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` observed with token wired. |\n| `executor_cancel_started` | `\"timeout\"` / `\"interrupt\"` | `{}` | Runner is about to call `executor.cancel()`. |\n| `executor_cancel_completed` | `\"timeout\"` / `\"interrupt\"` | `{}` | `cancel()` returned without raising. |\n| `executor_cancel_error` | `\"timeout\"` / `\"interrupt\"` | `{\"error_class\": str, \"message\": str}` | `cancel()` raised; failure is swallowed. |\n| `executor_cancel_skipped` | `\"timeout\"` / `\"interrupt\"` | `{\"reason\": str}` | Executor has no `cancel` method. |\n| `outcome` | `\"outcome\"` | `{\"status\": str, \"duration_sec\": float, \"error_class\": Optional[str]}` | Terminal classification (`INTERRUPTED` / `TIMEOUT` / `PASS` / `FAIL` / `ERRORED`). |\n\nExisting T03.05 events (`setup_*`, `spawn_*`, `inject_*`, `observe_*`,\n`assertion_*`, `teardown_*`) are unchanged.\n\n## 4. Exit Code Contract\n\n`EXIT_INTERRUPTED = 3` is exported so the CLI dispatcher (Phase 4) and\nthe orchestrator (T03.15) share a single source of truth. Mapping per\ndesign-spec §4:\n\n| Condition | Exit code |\n|---|---|\n| All evals PASS (or XFAIL / SKIPPED only) | 0 |\n| At least one FAIL / XPASS / TIMEOUT / ERRORED | 1 |\n| Harness setup error (schema, capability gate, scratch root) | 2 |\n| SIGINT / SIGTERM observed during a run | 3 |\n\nThis deliverable owns *only* the `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` constant + the\nCancellationToken/SignalHandlerInstaller wiring. The dispatcher that\ncalls `sys.exit(3)` lands in Phase 4.\n\n## 5. Out of Scope\n\n* **Partial-summary writing.** The reporter\n  (COMP-008 / T03.13) is responsible for persisting the interrupted run\n  summary. `SignalHandlerInstaller.on_signal` is the hook the\n  orchestrator can use to call into the reporter, but no concrete\n  writer ships here.\n* **Orchestrator wiring.** Allocating the shared `CancellationToken`,\n  installing the handler around the ThreadPoolExecutor, and translating\n  cancelled futures into outcomes belongs to T03.15.\n* **Retry policy.** NFR-REL2 (T03.08) builds on top of these\n  primitives.\n* **`signal.alarm`-based per-eval timeout.** The runner already uses a\n  `threading.Thread.join(timeout=...)` strategy (T03.05) that does not\n  hold the GIL or interfere with the main-thread signal handler.\n  Switching to `signal.alarm` would conflict with the signal handler\n  installer; we explicitly do not use it.\n\n## 6. Failure Modes & Containment\n\n* **Token cancelled mid-step.** The runner does not interrupt the\n  worker thread synchronously. The token's role is to (a) prevent the\n  orchestrator scheduling further evals and (b) let the runner detect\n  the worker's eventual `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` and convert\n  it to `INTERRUPTED`. PtyDriver kill is the lever that actually unblocks\n  a stuck `expect()` call.\n* **Executor `cancel()` raises.** Logged as `executor_cancel_error`;\n  TIMEOUT / INTERRUPTED outcome still returns. The zombie may persist,\n  but the harness exit will reap it via the daemon-thread\n  `Thread.join` semantics.\n* **Handler called from non-main thread.** `SignalHandlerInstaller`\n  refuses to install (Python restriction); the orchestrator must\n  install before spawning worker threads.\n* **Restoration failure.** Best-effort: `signal.signal` errors during\n  `restore()` are swallowed so harness shutdown is never blocked.\n\n## 7. Acceptance Criteria → Test Mapping\n\n| AC | Test |\n|---|---|\n| `CancellationToken` thread-safe one-shot + signum capture. | `TestCancellationToken::*` (10 tests) |\n| `SignalHandlerInstaller` install/restore/callback/main-thread guard. | `TestSignalHandlerInstaller::*` (8 tests) |\n| Pre-cancelled token → `INTERRUPTED` without spawning. | `test_pre_cancelled_token_returns_interrupted_without_spawn` |\n| Worker `KeyboardInterrupt` with token → `INTERRUPTED` outcome + executor cancel. | `test_keyboard_interrupt_with_token_returns_interrupted` |\n| Worker `KeyboardInterrupt` without token → propagates (regression). | `test_keyboard_interrupt_without_token_propagates` |\n| Per-eval timeout → `TIMEOUT` + `executor.cancel()` invoked, JSONL records `executor_cancel_*`. | `test_timeout_invokes_executor_cancel` |\n| `executor.cancel()` failure is swallowed; outcome still TIMEOUT. | `test_timeout_swallows_cancel_failure` |\n| Executor without `cancel` method gets `executor_cancel_skipped`. | `test_executor_without_cancel_logs_skipped` |\n| Real PtyDriver child killed and reaped (zombie reap AC). | `test_pty_driver_terminate_kills_real_subprocess` |\n\nSee `evidence.md` for the recorded pytest output.\n","numLines":114,"startLine":80,"totalLines":193}}}

Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    expect_id: str\n    expect_name: str\n    expected: Any\n    actual: Any\n    message: str = \"\"\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"title\",\n    \"status\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"expects\",\n    \"skip_reason\",\n    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error_class\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n      filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n      manifest at render time.\n    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n      raises ``ValueError``.\n    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n      the model accepts both shapes.\n    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n      identical harness failures across runs.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"","numLines":120,"startLine":200,"totalLines":931}}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01RfPc3U3bnSn4nTuriGhZJP","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01QREF9d8jnSFs7vFxW2UAM6","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/src/superclaude/cli/eval/orchestrator.py","content":"\"\"\"COMP-003 RunOrchestrator — parallel eval scheduler.\n\nTask T03.15 / Deliverable D-0057 / Roadmap row R-057 (COMP-003).\n\nThe orchestrator is the single component that turns a sequence of\nexpanded :class:`EvalSpec` rows into a sequence of :class:`EvalOutcome`\nrows by scheduling per-spec :class:`EvalRunner` workers through\n``concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed`` — the AC6\npattern the design-spec pins for cliEval. The implementation deliberately\nmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\nso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\nthe same scheduling skeleton.\n\nResponsibilities\n================\n\nThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\nintentionally defers:\n\n1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n   effective value.\n2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n   spec.\n3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n   into a status.\n\nThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\ninstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\nallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\nrunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n\nWorker contract\n===============\n\nThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\nitself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\nisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\nsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\ncaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\nthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\ntypically write::\n\n    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n        return runner.run(spec)\n\nThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\nmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\na canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n\nIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\nof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\nthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\nrecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. This keeps\nthe per-spec contract intact even when a worker misbehaves.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom concurrent.futures import Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom typing import Callable, ClassVar, Optional, Sequence\n\nfrom .models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\nfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n\n\n__all__ = [\"RunOrchestrator\", \"EvalWorker\"]\n\n\n# A worker callable receives an :class:`EvalSpec` and returns the\n# corresponding :class:`EvalOutcome`. Production wiring composes\n# ``EvalRunner.run`` with the per-spec resource allocation the\n# orchestrator deliberately does not own.\nEvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]\n\n\nclass RunOrchestrator:\n    \"\"\"Schedule per-spec eval workers in parallel via ``ThreadPoolExecutor``.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    run_one:\n        Worker callable invoked once per :class:`EvalSpec`. Returns the\n        :class:`EvalOutcome` for that spec. Worker exceptions are folded\n        into an ``ERRORED`` outcome — the orchestrator never re-raises\n        out of :meth:`run`.\n    cancellation_token:\n        Optional :class:`CancellationToken` shared with the CLI\n        dispatcher's signal handler (NFR-REL1 / T03.07). When the token\n        is set, the orchestrator stops submitting new work and\n        synthesises an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for every spec that was\n        never scheduled.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n    MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n    MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        run_one: EvalWorker,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not callable(run_one):\n            raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n        self._run_one = run_one\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(\n        self,\n        specs: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n        *,\n        parallel: int = DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n    ) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n        \"\"\"Execute every spec in parallel and return one outcome per spec.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        specs:\n            Expanded :class:`EvalSpec` sequence (post FR-EXP1 expansion).\n            The returned list preserves this order so the Reporter\n            renders rows deterministically.\n        parallel:\n            Requested concurrency level. Clamped to\n            ``[MIN_PARALLEL, MAX_PARALLEL]``; values below the minimum\n            raise :class:`ValueError`, values above the maximum saturate\n            to ``MAX_PARALLEL``. Defaults to :attr:`DEFAULT_PARALLEL`.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        list[EvalOutcome]\n            One :class:`EvalOutcome` per input spec, in the input order.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # The minimum-bound rejection is the only hard failure: a\n        # negative or zero concurrency value is almost certainly a\n        # caller bug (CLI argument parsing should have caught it\n        # earlier) and silently coercing it would mask the bug. An\n        # upper-bound value, by contrast, is the documented behaviour\n        # of design-spec §11 — \"parallel=20 clamps to 15\" — and the\n        # orchestrator must honour it without raising.\n        if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"parallel must be int; got {type(parallel).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"parallel must be >= {self.MIN_PARALLEL}; got {parallel}\"\n            )\n        max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n\n        if not specs:\n            return []\n\n        # Pre-allocate the result slots so we can fill them by index as\n        # futures complete. ``as_completed`` yields in completion order,\n        # not submission order, so we cannot append blindly.\n        outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the token\n        # was already cancelled when the scheduler reached them. They\n        # receive a synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome below.\n        cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n\n        with ThreadPoolExecutor(\n            max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n        ) as pool:\n            futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n\n            for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n                # Cooperative cancellation: once the token flips, stop\n                # submitting new work. Already-running workers observe\n                # the token themselves (via :class:`EvalRunner`) and\n                # convert to INTERRUPTED outcomes; we synthesise an\n                # INTERRUPTED row for every spec that did not even\n                # reach the pool so the per-spec contract still holds.\n                if self._is_cancelled():\n                    cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                    continue\n                future = pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)\n                futures[future] = index\n\n            # Drain the pool. ``as_completed`` is the AC6 idiom — it\n            # yields each future the instant its worker returns,\n            # letting downstream readers (telemetry, TUI) update in\n            # near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n            for future in as_completed(futures):\n                index = futures[future]\n                spec = specs[index]\n                try:\n                    outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — see docstring\n                    # The worker should fold its own harness errors\n                    # into an ERRORED outcome via :func:`run_eval`. We\n                    # only get here when the worker itself raised\n                    # (construction failure, unwired-token\n                    # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). Translate\n                    # to ERRORED so :meth:`run` never re-raises.\n                    outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n\n        # Backfill INTERRUPTED outcomes for never-submitted specs after\n        # the pool has shut down so we cannot race with an in-flight\n        # cancellation.\n        for index in cancelled_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n\n        # Every slot must be populated by now. The cast filters the\n        # ``Optional[...]`` typing without runtime cost; the assertion\n        # guards against a future regression.\n        assert all(o is not None for o in outcomes), (\n            \"RunOrchestrator: one or more outcome slots were left empty\"\n        )\n        return [o for o in outcomes if o is not None]\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _invoke_worker(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Indirection so subclasses / tests can hook the call site.\"\"\"\n        return self._run_one(spec)\n\n    def _is_cancelled(self) -> bool:\n        token = self._cancellation_token\n        return token is not None and token.is_cancelled()\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _interrupted_outcome(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build the synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for a skipped spec.\n\n        Mirrors :meth:`EvalRunner._make_interrupted_outcome` so the\n        reporter cannot distinguish an in-flight cancellation from an\n        orchestrator-level one — both shapes are byte-identical.\n        \"\"\"\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"INTERRUPTED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _errored_outcome(spec: EvalSpec, exc: BaseException) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Fold a worker exception into a DM-001 ``ERRORED`` outcome.\n\n        ``error_class`` is the fully-qualified exception classname so\n        the Reporter can group identical harness failures across runs\n        without parsing free-form messages. Matches the format\n        :func:`run_eval` uses for in-runner harness failures.\n        \"\"\"\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"ERRORED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n        )\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":113,"cache_read_input_tokens":151545,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":113,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":2,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"18f99fc2-4115-4065-b327-a6b449889f9a","uuid":"3179f8b1-0671-495a-983a-35b3a31062b0"}

When the token\n        is set, the orchestrator stops submitting new work and\n        synthesises an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for every spec that was\n        never scheduled.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n    MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n    MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        run_one: EvalWorker,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not callable(run_one):\n            raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n        self._run_one = run_one\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(\n        self,\n        specs: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n        *,\n        parallel: int = DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n    ) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n        \"\"\"Execute every spec in parallel and return one outcome per spec.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        specs:\n            Expanded :class:`EvalSpec` sequence (post FR-EXP1 expansion).\n            The returned list preserves this order so the Reporter\n            renders rows deterministically.\n        parallel:\n            Requested concurrency level. Clamped to\n            ``[MIN_PARALLEL, MAX_PARALLEL]``; values below the minimum\n            raise :class:`ValueError`, values above the maximum saturate\n            to ``MAX_PARALLEL``. Defaults to :attr:`DEFAULT_PARALLEL`.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        list[EvalOutcome]\n            One :class:`EvalOutcome` per input spec, in the input order.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # The minimum-bound rejection is the only hard failure: a\n        # negative or zero concurrency value is almost certainly a\n        # caller bug (CLI argument parsing should have caught it\n        # earlier) and silently coercing it would mask the bug. An\n        # upper-bound value, by contrast, is the documented behaviour\n        # of design-spec §11 — \"parallel=20 clamps to 15\" — and the\n        # orchestrator must honour it without raising.\n        if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"parallel must be int; got {type(parallel).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"parallel must be >= {self.MIN_PARALLEL}; got {parallel}\"\n            )\n        max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n\n        if not specs:\n            return []\n\n        # Pre-allocate the result slots so we can fill them by index as\n        # futures complete. ``as_completed`` yields in completion order,\n        # not submission order, so we cannot append blindly.\n        outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the token\n        # was already cancelled when the scheduler reached them. They\n        # receive a synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome below.\n        cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n\n        with ThreadPoolExecutor(\n            max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n        ) as pool:\n            futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n\n            for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n                # Cooperative cancellation: once the token flips, stop\n                # submitting new work. Already-running workers observe\n                # the token themselves (via :class:`EvalRunner`) and\n                # convert to INTERRUPTED outcomes; we synthesise an\n                # INTERRUPTED row for every spec that did not even\n                # reach the pool so the per-spec contract still holds.\n                if self._is_cancelled():\n                    cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                    continue\n                future = pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)\n                futures[future] = index\n\n            # Drain the pool. ``as_completed`` is the AC6 idiom — it\n            # yields each future the instant its worker returns,\n            # letting downstream readers (telemetry, TUI) update in\n            # near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n            for future in as_completed(futures):\n                index = futures[future]\n                spec = specs[index]\n                try:\n                    outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — see docstring\n                    # The worker should fold its own harness errors\n                    # into an ERRORED outcome via :func:`run_eval`. We\n                    # only get here when the worker itself raised\n                    # (construction failure, unwired-token\n                    # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). Translate\n                    # to ERRORED so :meth:`run` never re-raises.\n                    outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n\n        # Backfill INTERRUPTED outcomes for never-submitted specs after\n        # the pool has shut down so we cannot race with an in-flight\n        # cancellation.\n        for index in cancelled_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n\n        # Every slot must be populated by now. Matches the format\n        :func:`run_eval` uses for in-runner harness failures.\n        \"\"\"\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"ERRORED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n        )\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01He15aXohVk2s5TFWuEyarn","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_016eahEymHBx1QZ5Ks4BdFK2","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0057/spec.md","content":"# D-0057 — COMP-003 RunOrchestrator\n\n**Task:** T03.15 (Phase 3, Roadmap COMP-003 / R-057)\n**Module:** `src/superclaude/cli/eval/orchestrator.py`\n**Tests:** `tests/cli/eval/test_orchestrator.py`\n**Status:** Implemented 2026-05-20\n\n## 1. Goal\n\nSchedule per-spec `EvalRunner` workers in parallel via\n`concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed` — the AC6\npattern the cliEval design-spec pins (referenced shape:\n`src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`). The orchestrator owns\nthree cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer (T03.04) and runner\nclass (T03.05) deliberately deferred:\n\n1. **Concurrency budget** — `max_workers` clamped to `[1, 15]` per\n   design-spec §11 / NFR-PERF2 with default `8`.\n2. **Outcome contract** — one `EvalOutcome` per input `EvalSpec`, in\n   input order, regardless of completion order (the Reporter / FR-RPT1\n   N'-vs-K invariant relies on this).\n3. **Cooperative cancellation** — when the shared `CancellationToken`\n   (NFR-REL1 / T03.07) flips, scheduling stops and unsubmitted specs\n   synthesise an `INTERRUPTED` outcome so the per-spec contract still\n   holds.\n\nThe orchestrator does **not** install signal handlers. That belongs to\nthe CLI dispatcher (Phase 4): the dispatcher allocates a token, wraps\nthe orchestrator call in a `SignalHandlerInstaller`, and threads the\ntoken down so runners observe the same flag the signal handler flips.\n\n## 2. Public Surface\n\n```python\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.orchestrator import RunOrchestrator, EvalWorker\nfrom superclaude.cli.eval.signal_handler import CancellationToken\n```\n\n### 2.1 `EvalWorker`\n\nType alias:\n\n```python\nEvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]\n```\n\nA worker callable wraps everything from per-eval HOME allocation through\n`EvalRunner.run`. Keeping the orchestrator agnostic of construction\ndetails lets the CLI dispatcher own resource allocation and lets tests\nsubstitute a stub worker that returns a canned outcome.\n\n### 2.2 `RunOrchestrator`\n\n| Attribute / method | Contract |\n|---|---|\n| `DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8` | Default concurrency level. |\n| `MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1` | Minimum accepted `parallel`. Values below raise `ValueError`. |\n| `MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15` | Upper bound; values above saturate. |\n| `__init__(*, run_one, cancellation_token=None)` | Worker callable + optional token. Non-callable `run_one` raises `TypeError`. |\n| `run(specs, *, parallel=DEFAULT_PARALLEL) -> list[EvalOutcome]` | Schedule and collect outcomes. Empty `specs` → `[]`. |\n\n### 2.3 Outcome shape\n\nThe orchestrator emits two synthesised outcome shapes itself:\n\n| Shape | Trigger | Status | `duration_sec` | `error_class` |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Interrupted | Cancellation token set before spec submitted | `INTERRUPTED` | `0.0` | `None` |\n| Errored | Worker callable raised | `ERRORED` | `0.0` | `f\"{type(exc).__module__}.{type(exc).__qualname__}\"` |\n\nEvery other outcome shape is returned verbatim from the worker —\nnotably `PASS` / `FAIL` / `TIMEOUT` / `INTERRUPTED` from the in-runner\nclassification (D-0049 / D-0050).\n\n## 3. Scheduling Pattern\n\n```python\nwith ThreadPoolExecutor(\n    max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n) as pool:\n    futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n    for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n        if self._is_cancelled():\n            cancelled_indices.append(index)\n            continue\n        futures[pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)] = index\n\n    for future in as_completed(futures):\n        index = futures[future]\n        try:\n            outcomes[index] = future.result()\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n            outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(specs[index], exc)\n\nfor index in cancelled_indices:\n    outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n```\n\nNotes:\n\n* `outcomes` is pre-allocated and indexed by submission order so the\n  return-value order is independent of completion order.\n* The cancellation check is per-spec inside the submission loop, not\n  per-future. In-flight runners observe the same token via\n  `EvalRunner._cancellation_token` (D-0050) and convert worker-thread\n  `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` into `INTERRUPTED` outcomes\n  themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n  into a status.\n* `thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"` makes per-eval threads identifiable in\n  `py-spy` / `ps` output for diagnosing hangs.\n\n## 4. Parallelism Validation\n\n`tests/cli/eval/test_orchestrator.py::test_three_eval_suite_runs_faster_than_3x_sequential`\nasserts the runtime contract directly:\n\n* 3 specs × 0.2s sleep each.\n* `parallel=3`.\n* Elapsed must be `< 3 × eval_duration_sec` (i.e. `< 0.6s`). A truly\n  serialised execution would be ≥ 0.6s. The 2× margin absorbs\n  ThreadPoolExecutor startup overhead on slow CI hosts.\n\nConcurrency-bound tests\n(`test_parallel_above_max_clamps_to_fifteen`,\n`test_parallel_one_serialises`) snapshot the maximum number of workers\nrunning simultaneously to confirm the clamp is honoured at runtime, not\njust at validation time.\n\n## 5. Out of Scope\n\n* **Signal handler installation.** Phase 4 CLI dispatcher owns this.\n* **Partial summary writing.** Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) reads the\n  outcomes the orchestrator returns and persists `summary.{md,json}`.\n* **Exit-code translation.** Phase 4 dispatcher maps the outcome set\n  to `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` (`3`), `1`, `2`, or `0` per design-spec §4.\n* **Per-eval timeout enforcement.** Already in `EvalRunner` (D-0049 +\n  D-0050); the orchestrator never time-slices a single eval.\n* **15-eval isolation integration scenario.** T03.16 owns the end-to-end\n  proof; this deliverable owns only the scheduling primitive.\n\n## 6. Failure Modes & Containment\n\n| Mode | Behaviour |\n|---|---|\n| `parallel < 1` | `ValueError` raised before any submission. |\n| `parallel` not `int` (or `bool`) | `TypeError` raised. |\n| `parallel > 15` | Saturates to `MAX_PARALLEL = 15`. |\n| Worker raises | Folded into `ERRORED` outcome with `error_class`. `run()` never re-raises. |\n| Token pre-cancelled | No worker is submitted; every spec receives an `INTERRUPTED` outcome. |\n| Token cancelled mid-run | Specs already submitted run to completion (their in-runner observation of the same token converts them to `INTERRUPTED` if they have not finished); unsubmitted specs get synthesised `INTERRUPTED`. |\n| `run_one` not callable at construction | `TypeError` from `__init__`. |\n| Empty `specs` | Returns `[]` immediately; no pool started. |\n\n## 7. Acceptance Criteria → Test Mapping\n\n| AC | Test |\n|---|---|\n| One `EvalOutcome` per expanded spec, input order preserved. | `TestOneOutcomePerSpec::test_outcome_order_matches_input_order`, `TestOneOutcomePerSpec::test_every_spec_gets_exactly_one_outcome` |\n| `parallel=20` clamps to 15. | `TestParallelClamp::test_parallel_above_max_clamps_to_fifteen` |\n| `parallel < 1` rejected. | `TestParallelClamp::test_zero_parallel_rejected`, `TestParallelClamp::test_negative_parallel_rejected` |\n| 3-eval suite runs faster than 3× slowest-eval duration. | `test_three_eval_suite_runs_faster_than_3x_sequential` |\n| Pre-cancelled token → all `INTERRUPTED`, no worker invoked. | `TestCancellation::test_pre_cancelled_token_skips_all_specs` |\n| Mid-run cancel stops new submissions. | `TestCancellation::test_mid_run_cancel_stops_new_submissions` |\n| Worker exception folded into `ERRORED` outcome. | `TestWorkerExceptionFolding::test_runtime_error_from_worker_folds_to_errored` |\n| Constructor rejects non-callable worker. | `TestConstructorGuards::test_non_callable_worker_rejected` |\n\nSee `evidence.md` for the recorded pytest output.\n\n## 8. References\n\n* Reference pattern: `src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`\n  (PRD pipeline's parallel step scheduler).\n* Upstream contracts: D-0049 (EvalRunner class), D-0050 (signal\n  handling + cancellation token).\n* Downstream consumers: T03.16 (FR-G2 15-eval integration), Phase 4\n  CLI dispatcher (signal handler installation + exit-code translation),\n  COMP-008 Reporter (FR-RPT1 N'-vs-K invariant on the returned outcome\n  list).\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":233,"cache_read_input_tokens":164433,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":233,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":59,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"18f99fc2-4115-4065-b327-a6b449889f9a","uuid":"92f55a50-7717-4c7a-96d6-8d73809b181a"}

Key snippet:\n     ```python\n     class RunOrchestrator:\n         DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n         MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n         MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n\n         def __init__(self, *, run_one: EvalWorker,\n                      cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None) -> None:\n             if not callable(run_one):\n                 raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n             self._run_one = run_one\n             self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n\n         def run(self, specs, *, parallel=DEFAULT_PARALLEL) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n             if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n                 raise TypeError(...)\n             if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n                 raise ValueError(...)\n             max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n             if not specs: return []\n             outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n             cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n             with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers,\n                                     thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\") as pool:\n                 futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n                 for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n                     if self._is_cancelled():\n                         cancelled_indices.append(index); continue\n                     futures[pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)] = index\n                 for future in as_completed(futures):\n                     index = futures[future]; spec = specs[index]\n                     try:\n                         outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                     except BaseException as exc:\n                         outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n             for index in cancelled_indices:\n                 outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n             return [o for o in outcomes if o is not None]\n     ```\n     - `_interrupted_outcome` and `_errored_outcome` static helpers build DM-001 EvalOutcomes with `status=\"INTERRUPTED\"`/`\"ERRORED\"`, `duration_sec=0.0`\n   \n   - **CREATED**: `/config/workspace/IronClaude/tests/cli/eval/test_orchestrator.py`\n     - 20 tests across 6 classes: TestOneOutcomePerSpec, TestParallelClamp, test_three_eval_suite_runs_faster_than_3x_sequential, TestCancellation, TestWorkerExceptionFolding, TestConstructorGuards\n     - Uses stub workers (`Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`) instead of real EvalRunner instances\n     - Concurrency observed via running-counter pattern with locks\n     - Parallel speedup test: 3 specs × 0.2s sleep, parallel=3, elapsed must be < 0.6s\n   \n   - **CREATED**: `/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0057/spec.md`\n     - Comprehensive contract doc with 8 sections: Goal, Public Surface, Scheduling Pattern, Parallelism Validation, Out of Scope, Failure Modes, AC→Test Mapping, References\n\n4. Chose `EvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]` because (a) the orchestrator cannot resolve per-eval HOME isolation, paths, executor wiring; (b) it makes unit testing trivial; (c) the production call site composes `runner = EvalRunner(...); return runner.run(spec)` into one closure.\n   - **Order preservation**: `as_completed` yields in completion order, so the orchestrator pre-allocates `outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)` and indexes by submission position.\n   - **Worker exception folding**: Per-spec contract requires `len(outcomes) == len(specs)`, so worker exceptions become synthesized `ERRORED` outcomes (`run()` never re-raises).\n   - **Boolean rejection**: `bool` is an `int` subclass in Python; explicitly rejected with `isinstance(parallel, bool)` check to prevent silent coercion.\n\n6. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01S5HhrcWzWExeKYWjyrrGbb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_015BEffpi19siR8jKrozVrDe","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0057/notes.md","content":"# D-0057 RunOrchestrator — Design Notes\n\nCompanion to `spec.md`. These notes capture the design decisions made while\nimplementing T03.15 (COMP-003 `RunOrchestrator`), the alternatives considered,\nand the reasoning behind the contract chosen.\n\n## Why a worker callable instead of owning EvalRunner construction\n\nThe first sketch had `RunOrchestrator.__init__` take the wiring needed to build\nan `EvalRunner` per spec (home root, config, executor, deploy hooks, …) and\ninternally instantiate one runner per submitted spec. This was rejected for\nthree reasons:\n\n1. **Per-eval state the orchestrator cannot resolve.** `EvalRunner` requires a\n   per-eval HOME isolation directory, a per-eval `run_dir`/`artifacts_dir`, and\n   per-eval log file paths. Allocating those is the loader/runner layer's job\n   (T03.05/T03.07), not the scheduler's. Pushing the allocation into\n   `RunOrchestrator` would couple it to filesystem layout decisions that live\n   outside D-0057.\n2. **Testability.** With the worker callable contract\n   (`EvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`), the orchestrator's unit\n   tests use trivial stub workers (lambdas, classes with `__call__`,\n   instrumented closures that record concurrency) without needing to stand up a\n   real isolated HOME, pty pipeline, or hook deploy. The 20 tests in\n   `tests/cli/eval/test_orchestrator.py` therefore exercise scheduling\n   behaviour in isolation from runner internals.\n3. **Composition over configuration.** The production call site (Phase 5\n   `superclaude eval` CLI) composes the worker once:\n\n   ```python\n   def worker(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n       runner = EvalRunner(home=..., config=..., executor=..., run_dir=...,\n                          artifacts_dir=..., stdout_path=..., stderr_path=...,\n                          transcript_path=..., expect_callables=...,\n                          deploy_hooks=..., default_timeout_sec=...,\n                          clock=..., cancellation_token=token, retry_count=0)\n       return runner.run(spec)\n\n   orchestrator = RunOrchestrator(run_one=worker, cancellation_token=token)\n   outcomes = orchestrator.run(specs, parallel=parallel)\n   ```\n\n   This keeps the orchestrator focused on the one thing it owns: the\n   `ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed` scheduling loop.\n\n## Why ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed (and not asyncio)\n\nThe release spec (`AC6`) and `cli/prd/executor.py:774-802` both prescribe the\nsynchronous `ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed` pattern. `EvalRunner` itself is\nblocking (pty I/O, subprocess.Popen, signal handling), so an asyncio bridge\nwould add complexity without parallelism gains. Threads share the GIL but the\nblocking time is dominated by I/O wait, so threads are sufficient.\n\n## Why pre-allocate `outcomes[None] * len(specs)` instead of appending\n\n`as_completed` yields futures in **completion** order, not submission order.\nThe `RunOrchestrator.run()` contract is that the returned list of outcomes\npreserves the order of the input `specs`. Pre-allocating a list and indexing by\nsubmission position is the simplest way to satisfy both constraints without\nsorting at the end. `test_outcome_order_matches_input_order` covers this.\n\n## Why fold worker exceptions into `ERRORED` outcomes\n\nThe N'-vs-K invariant (`len(outcomes) == counts.expanded_n_prime`, FR-RPT1)\nrequires one outcome per spec. If a worker raised, propagating the exception\nwould break the invariant and force the CLI layer to reconcile a partial list.\nInstead, `RunOrchestrator._errored_outcome` synthesises a minimal\n`EvalOutcome(status=\"ERRORED\", duration_sec=0.0, error_class=<exc class name>)`\nand the schedule loop continues. The CLI/reporter layer downstream sees a\nclean per-spec list and can render the error class in the report.\n\nA separate question is whether `KeyboardInterrupt`/`SystemExit` should fold or\npropagate. The current implementation catches `BaseException` so the loop\nfinishes draining (other in-flight workers still get a chance to return their\noutcomes); cancellation propagates through the `CancellationToken`, not through\nexception propagation. Tests `test_runtime_error_from_worker_folds_to_errored`\nand the cancellation suite cover both paths.\n\n## Why pre-flight cancellation check before submission\n\n`_is_cancelled()` is checked twice in the schedule loop:\n\n1. **Before submission** — if the token fires while specs are being submitted,\n   all remaining specs are appended to `cancelled_indices` and synthesised as\n   `INTERRUPTED` outcomes after the pool drains. This avoids submitting work\n   that will immediately be cancelled.\n2. **In-flight** — already-submitted workers see the cancellation token via\n   `EvalRunner`'s own cooperative cancel path; the orchestrator does not\n   interrupt them externally.\n\nThe `test_mid_run_cancel_stops_new_submissions` test demonstrates this: with\nparallel=2 and 5 specs, after the first batch completes the token fires; the\nalready-submitted-but-not-yet-completed work finishes, and the rest is\nsynthesised as `INTERRUPTED`.\n\n## Why reject `bool` parallel explicitly\n\nIn Python, `bool` is a subclass of `int`, so `isinstance(True, int)` is\n`True` and `True < 1` is `False`. Without an explicit `isinstance(parallel,\nbool)` guard, `RunOrchestrator.run(specs, parallel=True)` would silently\ncoerce to `parallel=1`. The explicit guard surfaces the type confusion at the\ncall site (`TypeError`) rather than degrading the scheduler to single-threaded\noperation. Test: `test_boolean_parallel_rejected`.\n\n## Why MIN=1 and MAX=15 (not 0 and unbounded)\n\n- **MIN=1**: `parallel=0` would deadlock `ThreadPoolExecutor` (no workers, no\n  progress). The release spec range `[1, 15]` excludes zero. Negative values\n  are nonsensical for a worker count.\n- **MAX=15**: NFR-PERF2 caps RAM at ≤2.25GB. With each EvalRunner needing up\n  to 150MB peak (pty + python + hook subprocess working set in worst-case),\n  15 × 150MB ≈ 2.25GB. The clamp is a hard guarantee — even if the CLI\n  passes `parallel=20` (T03.10's `--parallel` flag accepts up to 20 for\n  forward-compat), the scheduler refuses to spawn more than 15 threads.\n\n## Out-of-scope items deliberately not implemented\n\nThese belong in other tasks; D-0057 scope ends at the scheduling primitive:\n\n- **Progress reporting** — JSONL emission is FR-RPT2's job; the orchestrator\n  returns a list, it does not stream events.\n- **Retry policy** — NFR-REL2 says no retries. The orchestrator does not loop;\n  worker exceptions become `ERRORED` immediately.\n- **Signal handler installation** — `SignalHandlerInstaller` is set up by the\n  CLI entry point (Phase 4 task), the orchestrator accepts the resulting\n  token.\n- **`EvalRunner` construction** — handled by the CLI wiring layer (see \"Why a\n  worker callable\" above).\n\n## Reference implementation\n\n- Pattern source: `src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`\n  (`run_in_parallel` method on `PRDExecutor` — same `ThreadPoolExecutor +\n  as_completed` loop, same `shutdown_requested` pre-flight pattern, same\n  exception folding shape).\n- Spec source: `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0050/spec.md`\n  (NFR-REL1 cancellation contract — orchestrator allocates token, installs\n  handler around pool, T03.15 owns the wiring).\n\n## Verification evidence\n\nSee `evidence.md` in this directory for verbatim pytest output. Summary:\n\n- 20/20 orchestrator unit tests pass in 0.48s.\n- 56/56 regression tests pass across orchestrator + runner_class + signal_handling\n  in 2.06s.\n- No regressions in the broader suite.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":356,"cache_read_input_tokens":71280,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":356,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":50,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"18f99fc2-4115-4065-b327-a6b449889f9a","uuid":"7ca237e0-3431-4d5b-80d1-396bcdef9742"}

The release spec range `[1, 15]` excludes zero. Negative values\n  are nonsensical for a worker count.\n- **MAX=15**: NFR-PERF2 caps RAM at ≤2.25GB. With each EvalRunner needing up\n  to 150MB peak (pty + python + hook subprocess working set in worst-case),\n  15 × 150MB ≈ 2.25GB. The clamp is a hard guarantee — even if the CLI\n  passes `parallel=20` (T03.10's `--parallel` flag accepts up to 20 for\n  forward-compat), the scheduler refuses to spawn more than 15 threads.\n\n## Out-of-scope items deliberately not implemented\n\nThese belong in other tasks; D-0057 scope ends at the scheduling primitive:\n\n- **Progress reporting** — JSONL emission is FR-RPT2's job; the orchestrator\n  returns a list, it does not stream events.\n- **Retry policy** — NFR-REL2 says no retries. The orchestrator does not loop;\n  worker exceptions become `ERRORED` immediately.\n- **Signal handler installation** — `SignalHandlerInstaller` is set up by the\n  CLI entry point (Phase 4 task), the orchestrator accepts the resulting\n  token.\n- **`EvalRunner` construction** — handled by the CLI wiring layer (see \"Why a\n  worker callable\" above).\n\n## Reference implementation\n\n- Pattern source: `src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`\n  (`run_in_parallel` method on `PRDExecutor` — same `ThreadPoolExecutor +\n  as_completed` loop, same `shutdown_requested` pre-flight pattern, same\n  exception folding shape).\n- Spec source: `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0050/spec.md`\n  (NFR-REL1 cancellation contract — orchestrator allocates token, installs\n  handler around pool, T03.15 owns the wiring).\n\n## Verification evidence\n\nSee `evidence.md` in this directory for verbatim pytest output. Summary:\n\n- 20/20 orchestrator unit tests pass in 0.48s.\n- 56/56 regression tests pass across orchestrator + runner_class + signal_handling\n  in 2.06s.\n- No regressions in the broader suite.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}
